After verifying the requested `answer_position` and active sheet once, export and stop; do not repeatedly re-import/export just to probe focus state.
[stderr] - When the requested result contains formulas, totals, calculated fields, dates, percentages, currencies, identifiers, or formatting-sensitive values, verify the workbook cell model before export with `getCellDatas()`: compare `v`, `t`, `f`, `p`, `si`, `s` or resolved style, and visible display where relevant. Do not use `getValues()` to verify value type. Do not replace required formulas with static calculated values unless the instruction explicitly asks for values only.
[stderr] - `inspect` and `pipe out` are useful only for quickly understanding visible content, rough ranges, and formulas. They cannot prove value type or complete cell model. `pipe out --type rawValue` is not facade `getRawValues()`; currently it maps to `range.getValues()`. Do not infer true `v`, `t`, `f`, `s`, date serials, forced text, or number formats from `inspect`, `pipe out`, TSV/CSV/JSON previews, or display values. For value type and model verification, always use `univer run` with `range.getCellDatas()`.
[stderr] - When verifying exact row numbers, section boundaries, TOTAL rows, blank separator rows, or formula ranges, bare `pipe out --format tsv/csv`, large-range `inspect range`, and `inspect formulas` Formula Groups are easy to misread: TSV/CSV has no row numbers and blank rows are hard to count, inspect previews can omit middle rows, and Formula Groups are summaries rather than per-cell formulas. Prefer `pipe out --format json` only for quick visible inspection; if exact coordinates, formulas, or cell models matter, use `univer run` to return explicit `{ row1, col1, value, formula, cellData }` objects from `getCellDatas()`.
[stderr] - In Univer run scripts, `sheet.getLastRow()` and `sheet.getLastColumn()` return 0-based last used row/column indexes. Numeric `sheet.getRange(row, column, numRows, numColumns)` uses 0-based start row/column parameters, while `numRows` and `numColumns` are counts, not end indexes.
[stderr] - Before writing to columns or rows outside the current sheet bounds, extend the sheet first and create the range only after extension. `getRange()` validates the current `rowCount` and `columnCount` when the range object is created; out-of-bounds ranges fail immediately. Use `setColumnCount(requiredColumnCount)` when you only need to make far-right columns writable. Use `insertColumns()`, `insertColumnsBefore()`, or `insertColumnsAfter()` only when the task requires inserting columns and shifting existing data.
[stderr] - For background color tasks, use facade style APIs instead of guessing style internals: write with `range.setBackgroundColor('#ffff00')` or `range.setBackground('#ffff00')`, and verify with `range.getBackground()` or `range.getBackgrounds()`. Do not rely only on `getCellDatas()` / `cellData.s` to decide whether a background color succeeded.
[stderr] - For rich text or partial text highlighting, use the official rich text builder API. Example:
[stderr]   ```ts
[stderr]   const richText = univerAPI.newRichText()
[stderr]     .insertText('Hello World')
[stderr]     .setStyle(0, 5, { bl: 1, cl: { rgb: '#ff0000' } });
[stderr]   sheet.getRange('A1').setRichTextValueForCell(richText);
[stderr]   ```
[stderr]   For ranges, use `setRichTextValues([[richText, richText]])`. Read rich text with `getValue(true)` or `getValues(true)` when needed. Do not hand-write or guess `cell.p.body.textRuns` unless you are only diagnosing an existing file structure.
[stderr] - `getRawValues()` returns stored values as-is: rich text returns `cell.p.body.dataStream`, otherwise it returns `cell.v`. It does not trim text, normalize NBSP (`\u00A0`), remove commas, or convert numeric-looking strings to numbers. For comparisons, blank detection, sums, sorting, and filters, normalize explicitly:
[stderr]   ```ts
[stderr]   const cleanText = (v) => String(v ?? '').replace(/\u00A0/g, ' ').replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').replace(/\r/g, '\n').trim();
[stderr]   const isBlank = (v) => cleanText(v) === '';
[stderr]   const parseNumberLoose = (v) => {
[stderr]     const s = cleanText(v).replace(/,/g, '');
[stderr]     if (s === '') return null;
[stderr]     const n = Number(s);
[stderr]     return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : null;
[stderr]   };
[stderr]   ```
[stderr]   Use normalization for internal logic, but do not blindly trim values written back when the task requires preserving original text, trailing spaces, or exact formatting.
[stderr] - Univer Sheets cell value rules:
[stderr]   1. The real cell model is `ICellData`, not a plain JavaScript value. Common fields include `v` (value), `t` (type enum), `f` (formula), `si` (formula id), `p` (rich text), `s` (style id or style object, including number format), and `custom` (custom data). The `t` enum values are: `1=STRING`, `2=NUMBER`, `3=BOOLEAN`, `4=FORCE_STRING`. If `t` is omitted, Univer may auto-detect the type, which can turn text like `2-2` into a date serial.
[stderr]   2. `getValues()` is not a universal raw read. It returns facade/view-layer values that may be affected by number formats, interceptors, and display logic, and it does not preserve `t`, `f`, `p`, `s`, or `custom`. Do not use `getValues()` to verify value type, formula, number format, forced text, or cell model.
[stderr]   3. Choose reads by intent: display-only planning may use `getValues()` or `getDisplayValues()`; raw values without model preservation may use `getRawValues()` for calculations; complete cell models and all value-type verification must use `getCellDatas()`; formulas use `getFormula()` or `getFormulas()`, and use `getCellDatas()` when formula cells also need model/type/format verification.
[stderr]   4. Hard requirement for writes: whenever you write values into cells or ranges, use explicit `ICellData` objects instead of bare JavaScript values. Do not write `setValues([["2-2", 123, true]])`; write `setValues([[{ v: "2-2", t: 4 }, { v: 123, t: 2 }, { v: 1, t: 3 }]])`. Every written cell must make its semantic type explicit with `t`, `f`, and any needed `s` number format. Do not use `pipe in` for workbook edits because it writes tabular values without an explicit per-cell `ICellData` model.
[stderr]   5. Choose write models by semantics: ordinary text uses `{ v: "text", t: 1 }`; text that must not be auto-converted uses `{ v: "2-2", t: 4 }`; numbers use `{ v: 123, t: 2 }`; booleans use `{ v: 1, t: 3 }` for TRUE and `{ v: 0, t: 3 }` for FALSE; formulas use `{ f: '=A1+B1' }` or `setFormula('=A1+B1')`; literal formula text uses `{ v: '=A1+B1', t: 4 }`.
[stderr]   6. Values that look like dates, numbers, fractions, scores, ranges, codes, phone numbers, postal codes, or identifiers must use `t: 4` when the task semantics require text. Examples include scores like `2-2` or `3-1`, hyphenated codes, leading-zero IDs, SKU/account IDs, phone-like values, and ZIP/postal codes. Do not let Excel/OOXML auto-detect these as dates or numbers.
[stderr]   7. Dates, percentages, and currencies are numbers plus number formats, not separate value types: date `{ v: serial, t: 2, s: { n: { pattern: 'yyyy-mm-dd' } } }`; percent `{ v: 0.25, t: 2, s: { n: { pattern: '0%' } } }`; currency `{ v: 1234.5, t: 2, s: { n: { pattern: '$#,##0.00' } } }`. After writing, `getCellDatas()` may show `s` as a style id string instead of an inline object; use `getCellStyleData()` / `getCellStyles()` or the workbook snapshot styles to resolve and verify the number format.
[stderr]   8. When reading existing cells and writing them elsewhere: display-only reads may use `getValues()`/`getDisplayValues()` only for internal planning, but writes must still use explicit `ICellData`; preserving type, formula, format, rich text, or custom data requires `getCellDatas()` -> deep clone -> clear target range -> `setValues()`; moving ranges should prefer a move-range command or `moveRows`/`moveColumns`.
[stderr]   9. If `p` rich text exists, it controls the displayed content even when `v` is also set. Use the rich text builder APIs for rich text tasks and verify `p` with `getCellDatas()`.
[stderr]   10. Do not hand-author `si` formula ids. Preserve `si` only when deep-cloning existing formula cells with `getCellDatas()`; for new formulas use `f` or `setFormula()`.
[stderr]   11. Hard requirement for row copy/extract/move tasks: if the instruction says to copy, extract, move, maintain formatting, preserve formatting, keep date formatting, or keep number formatting, do NOT use `getValues()`/`getDisplayValues()` -> `setValues()` for the copied cells. Use `getCellDatas()` with a deep clone, or explicitly write complete `ICellData` models for dates/numbers/formulas/formats. Verify at least one copied date/number/formula cell by raw value and type/model, not just by display text.
[stderr]   12. To clear cells, use `clearContent()` for contents only or `clear()` for contents plus formatting. Do not use `setValue(null)`.

[stderr] 
[stderr] # univer-cli
[stderr] 
[stderr] `univer` is a spreadsheet engine in the terminal. Use it when an agent needs real workbook semantics: sheets, ranges, formulas, formatting, layout, previews, imports, exports, or versioned workbook state.
[stderr] 
[stderr] Install the CLI with `npm i -g univer-cli`. Update the CLI with `univer update`. The executable is `univer`; `unv` may be available as a short alias.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Core Mental Model
[stderr] 
[stderr] Treat workbook-visible state as the source of truth. A successful command summary, package metadata, or an internal manifest does not prove that sheet names, cell values, formulas, formatting, or exported handoff files are correct.
[stderr] 
[stderr] The workbook path is the local identity. Pick one explicit path such as `./budget.univer` and use that path as the CLI target. Do not target workbooks by `unitId`, `sessionId`, manifest ids, or runtime ids.
[stderr] 
[stderr] `.univer` and `.unv` files are CLI operation targets, not agent-editable data stores. Read and write workbook data through public CLI surfaces such as `inspect`, `search`, `pipe`, `run`, `export`, `status`, and `commit`.
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use `univer help` and `univer help <command...>` for exact syntax. For `run` scripts, use `univer help run` and `univer help run <topic>` before relying on unfamiliar APIs.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Use When
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use this skill when the task involves spreadsheet or workbook work, especially:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - creating, importing, exporting, or handing off `.xlsx`, `.csv`, `.univer`, or `.unv` files
[stderr] - inspecting workbook shape, sheets, ranges, formulas, formatting, or visible cell state
[stderr] - locating content-defined rows, columns, headers, or cells before editing
[stderr] - making bounded edits to cells, formulas, formatting, charts, shapes, floating images, layout, or sheet structure
[stderr] - streaming rectangular workbook data through shell tools before reading it into context
[stderr] - writing generated matrix data back into a sheet-qualified range
[stderr] - previewing workbook state locally with `univer view`
[stderr] - reading submitted local viewer review comments with `univer view comments`
[stderr] - creating, restoring, resetting, pulling, or syncing local workbook changesets
[stderr] - proving that a workbook-visible mutation or export is correct enough to hand back
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Default Operating Loop
[stderr] 
[stderr] 1. Pick one explicit workbook path, for example `./budget.univer`.
[stderr] 2. Create or import a workbook first if no `.univer` or `.unv` target exists.
[stderr] 3. Inspect workbook-visible state before deciding where to write.
[stderr] 4. Locate targets from visible headers, values, formulas, or inspected ranges.
[stderr] 5. Choose the smallest public CLI surface that fits the task.
[stderr] 6. Mutate through the CLI, not by editing package internals.
[stderr] 7. Verify changed workbook-visible state with `inspect`, `pipe out`, or another public read.
[stderr] 8. Export only after verification when the user needs a handoff file.
[stderr] 9. After changes have been verified, if the user may need to inspect, audit, or review the final workbook, run `univer view "$WB" --no-open --json` to get a local preview link and include that link in your response.
[stderr] 10. Commit or sync only after verified changes when versioning is part of the workflow.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Hard Rules
[stderr] 
[stderr] - Do not read `.univer` or `.unv` internals to infer workbook contents.
[stderr] - Do not write, patch, unzip, rezip, rename internal files, or manipulate workbook package contents.
[stderr] - Do not inspect `manifest.json`, snapshots, mutation logs, or package fragments as a substitute for workbook-visible reads.
[stderr] - Do not guess sheet names, row numbers, formulas, ranges, or changed cells from memory or file metadata.
[stderr] - Do not treat stdout summaries as proof of workbook state. Verify with a workbook-visible read.
[stderr] - Do not invent commands or `run` APIs. Check `univer help` and documented run topics.
[stderr] 
[stderr] Direct package access can corrupt workbooks or teach the agent false state. If the CLI cannot read what you need, diagnose the CLI/runtime path instead of bypassing it.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Command Selection
[stderr] 
[stderr] | Need | Prefer |
[stderr] | --- | --- |
[stderr] | Discover exact command syntax | `univer help`, `univer help <command...>` |
[stderr] | Start a workbook package | `univer new` or `univer import` |
[stderr] | Hand back Excel-compatible output | `univer export` |
[stderr] | Understand workbook shape before editing | `univer inspect workbook`, then `univer inspect range` |
[stderr] | Locate content-defined cells | `univer search`, scoped with `--sheet` and/or `--range` when possible |
[stderr] | Stream rectangular data through shell tools | `univer pipe out` |
[stderr] | Write a known rectangular matrix back | `univer pipe in` |
[stderr] | Apply bounded workbook-local logic | `univer run --file` |
[stderr] | Create or maintain workbook charts | `univer run --file` with `univer help run charts` |
[stderr] | Create or maintain workbook shapes and connectors | `univer run --file` with `univer help run shapes` |
[stderr] | Create or maintain workbook floating images | `univer run --file` with `univer help run images` |
[stderr] | Preview readonly workbook state | `univer view --no-open --json` or `univer view` |
[stderr] | Read local viewer review feedback | `univer view comments "$WB" --json` |
[stderr] | Check local versioning state | `univer status` |
[stderr] | Create a local changeset from local mutations | `univer commit --message <message>` |
[stderr] | Discard uncommitted local mutations | `univer restore` |
[stderr] | Reset local unsynced commits | `univer reset --soft HEAD~N` or `univer reset --hard HEAD~N` |
[stderr] | Initialize a local package from an existing remote unit | `univer clone --unit-id <unitID>` |
[stderr] | Pull remote-only changes for a bound package | `univer pull` |
[stderr] | Sync local and remote versioning state | `univer sync` |
[stderr] | Diagnose runtime problems | `univer doctor`, `univer daemon status` |
[stderr] | Prepare a bug report or Univer team support artifact after user authorization | `univer doctor collect` |
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use canonical command help such as `univer help inspect range` and `univer help pipe out`. Top-level help group headings are visual sections only; do not run group-prefixed topics such as `univer help read inspect range`.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Execution Results
[stderr] 
[stderr] Treat non-zero exit as failure even when stdout is partially present. Read stderr before changing approach; it usually contains the stable diagnostic code, usage, and retry examples.
[stderr] 
[stderr] Keep data stdout clean in shell pipelines. If diagnostics are needed, capture stderr separately so downstream tools receive only workbook data.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] univer inspect range "$WB" --range 'Sheet1!A1:D20' > ./range.md 2> ./range.err
[stderr] status=$?
[stderr] if [ "$status" -ne 0 ]; then
[stderr]   sed -n '1,80p' ./range.err
[stderr]   exit "$status"
[stderr] fi
[stderr] sed -n '1,40p' ./range.md
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Workflow Recipes
[stderr] 
[stderr] These are verified command shapes. Replace paths, sheet names, and ranges with inspected workbook facts.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ### Create Or Import, Then Inspect
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] WB=./orders.univer
[stderr] univer import ./orders.csv "$WB" --json
[stderr] univer inspect workbook "$WB"
[stderr] univer inspect range "$WB" --range 'Sheet1!A1:D4'
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use `new` when the task starts from a blank workbook:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] WB=./workbook.univer
[stderr] univer new "$WB" --name "Workbook"
[stderr] univer inspect workbook "$WB"
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ### Locate Before Editing
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use `search` before editing when the target is defined by visible workbook content:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] univer search "$WB" West
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Plain text output is one A1 reference per matched cell, which keeps shell pipelines clean. With no
[stderr] matches, stdout is empty and the command still succeeds. Use `--json` when you need match metadata,
[stderr] the matched cell data preview, truncation flags, counts, or the searched scope:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] univer search "$WB" West --sheet Orders --range 'A1:Z200' --json
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] By default, search checks `displayValue`, so formatted values such as dates are searchable by the
[stderr] same strings a user sees in the sheet. Use `--type rawValue` for underlying numeric/string values
[stderr] such as spreadsheet date serials, and `--type formula` for formulas:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] univer search "$WB" 2024-01-01
[stderr] univer search "$WB" 45292 --type rawValue
[stderr] univer search "$WB" SUM --type formula --json
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Useful narrowing options:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `--sheet <name>` may be repeated.
[stderr] - `--range <A1>` may be repeated; unqualified ranges require a `--sheet`.
[stderr] - `--case-sensitive` and `--whole-cell` tighten matching.
[stderr] - `--max-results` limits returned matches; truncation is reported on stderr for text output and in JSON fields for `--json`.
[stderr] - `--max-cell-value-length` bounds previewed cell data in JSON.
[stderr] 
[stderr] If `search` fails, read stderr for the diagnostic message before changing approach. If the target is
[stderr] not content-defined, inspect a bounded range and derive the edit boundary from visible headers and
[stderr] sample rows:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] univer inspect range "$WB" --range 'Sheet1!A1:D20'
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ### Pipe Out Through Shell Tools
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use `pipe out` when the shell can reduce or reshape rectangular data before the agent reads it. Prefer TSV for `awk`, JSON for `jq`, and `--type rawValue` when formatted display text is not safe enough for comparisons.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] univer pipe out "$WB" --range 'Sheet1!A1:D4' --format tsv > ./orders.tsv
[stderr] awk -F '\t' 'BEGIN{OFS="\t"} NR==1 || $2=="West" {print $1,$3}' ./orders.tsv > ./west.tsv
[stderr] sed -n '1,5p' ./west.tsv
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ### Pipe In Generated Table Data
[stderr] 
[stderr] Write only a known matrix into an explicit, sheet-qualified range. Make `pipe in` the terminal stage of a pipeline unless you intentionally want its success summary downstream.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] univer pipe in "$WB" --range 'Sheet1!F1:G3' --input-format tsv --data-file ./west.tsv
[stderr] univer inspect range "$WB" --range 'Sheet1!F1:G3'
[stderr] univer pipe out "$WB" --range 'Sheet1!F1:G3' --format tsv
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Verify headers, sample rows, and key columns. Row count alone is weak evidence because shifted columns can still preserve row count.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ### Run A Bounded Workbook Script
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use `run --file` for workbook-native logic that does not fit `inspect`, `search`, `pipe`, or `export`. Check `univer help run` and the relevant `univer help run <topic>` manual before using unfamiliar APIs.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```bash
[stderr] cat > ./review.js <<'JS'
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Sheet1");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: "Sheet1 not found" };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("I1:J3").setValues([
[stderr]     ["metric", "value"],
[stderr]     ["west_orders", 2],
[stderr]     ["reviewed", "yes"],
[stderr]   ]);
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return { success: true, changedRanges: ["Sheet1!I1:J3"] };
[stderr] }
[stderr] JS
[stderr]

[stderr]  succeeded in 274ms:
[stderr] # Ranges
[stderr] 
[stderr] This topic groups cell and range operations for ordinary `run` tasks.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Reading From FRange
[stderr] 
[stderr] Get a range from `sheet.getRange(...)`, then use these reads:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `getValue() -> any` - get the top-left cell value as the normal displayed value
[stderr] - `getRawValue() -> any` - get the top-left cell raw underlying value
[stderr] - `getValues() -> any[][]` - get all cell values in the range as a 2D array
[stderr] - `getRawValues() -> any[][]` - get all underlying raw values in the range as a 2D array
[stderr] - `getFormulas() -> string[][]` - get formulas in A1 notation
[stderr] - `getA1Notation(withSheet?) -> string` - get the range A1 notation; `withSheet=true` includes the sheet name
[stderr] - `forEach(callback) -> void` - iterate cells in the range
[stderr] - `isMerged() -> boolean` - check whether the range itself is merged
[stderr] - `isPartOfMerge() -> boolean` - check whether the range falls inside a merged region
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Sales");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Sales" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   const range = sheet.getRange("A1:C3");
[stderr]   return {
[stderr]     success: true,
[stderr]     a1: range.getA1Notation(),
[stderr]     values: range.getValues(),
[stderr]     formulas: range.getFormulas()
[stderr]   };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Writing To FRange
[stderr] 
[stderr] Core writes:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `setValue(value) -> FRange` - set one value across the target range; if the value starts with `=`, it can also write a formula
[stderr] - `setValues(values) -> FRange` - batch set values from a 2D array
[stderr] - `setFormula(formula) -> void` - set a formula in A1 notation such as `=SUM(A1:B10)`
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Sheet1");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Sheet1" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("A1").setValue("Total");
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("B2:C3").setValues([
[stderr]     [10, 20],
[stderr]     [30, 40]
[stderr]   ]);
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return { success: true };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## setValues() Shape Rules
[stderr] 
[stderr] `setValues()` expects a rectangular 2D array whose shape matches the target range:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - one row per worksheet row in the target range
[stderr] - one column per worksheet column in the target range
[stderr] - keep every inner array the same length
[stderr] - use `getRange('A1:C10')` or `getRange(0, 0, 10, 3)` to define the full target shape
[stderr] - `getRange(row, col)` without size only targets a single cell
[stderr] 
[stderr] Correct:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] sheet.getRange("A1:B2").setValues([
[stderr]   ["Name", "Score"],
[stderr]   ["Ada", 95]
[stderr] ]);
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Incorrect shape:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] sheet.getRange("A1:B2").setValues([["Name"], ["Ada", 95, "extra"]]);
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] When writing a single cell, use `setValue()` unless the task specifically benefits from 2D shape handling.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Formulas And Async Readback
[stderr] 
[stderr] Formula calculation is asynchronous. This applies to:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `range.setFormula('=SUM(B1:B10)')`
[stderr] - `range.setValue('=SUM(B1:B10)')`
[stderr] 
[stderr] If you need the calculated result in the same `run`, wait for formula application:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] async () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Sales");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Sales" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("A1").setFormula("=SUM(B1:B10)");
[stderr]   await univerAPI.getFormula().onCalculationResultApplied();
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return {
[stderr]     success: true,
[stderr]     value: sheet.getRange("A1").getValue()
[stderr]   };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Available formula wait API:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `univerAPI.getFormula() -> FFormula`
[stderr] - `onCalculationResultApplied() -> Promise<void>` - wait for formula-calculation results to be applied before reading the computed value
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Merge And Unmerge
[stderr] 
[stderr] Merge APIs:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `merge(options?) -> void` - merge cells into one block
[stderr] - `breakApart() -> void` - unmerge cells
[stderr] - `mergeAcross() -> void` - merge each row across the selected columns
[stderr] - `mergeVertically() -> void` - merge each column down the selected rows
[stderr] 
[stderr] Typical uses:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `merge()` for one combined block
[stderr] - `mergeAcross()` to merge each row across the selected columns
[stderr] - `mergeVertically()` to merge each column down the selected rows
[stderr] - `breakApart()` to remove merges
[stderr] - use `merge({ isForceMerge: true })` if you need to clear overlapping merges first
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Report");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Report" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("A1:D1").merge();
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("A3:D4").mergeAcross();
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return {
[stderr]     success: true,
[stderr]     titleMerged: sheet.getRange("A1").isPartOfMerge()
[stderr]   };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Clear And Cell Shift Operations
[stderr] 
[stderr] Clear APIs:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `clear() -> void` - clear the full range
[stderr] - `clearContent() -> void` - clear only content and keep formatting
[stderr] - `clearFormat() -> void` - clear only formatting and keep content
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use `clear()` when the task is to clear the full range. Use `clearContent()` or `clearFormat()` when you only want one part of that behavior.
[stderr] 
[stderr] Cell shift APIs:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `insertCells(dimension) -> void` - insert cells and shift existing cells in the given dimension
[stderr] - `deleteCells(dimension) -> void` - delete cells and shift remaining cells in the given dimension
[stderr] 
[stderr] Dimension constants:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `univerAPI.Enum.Dimension.ROWS`
[stderr] - `univerAPI.Enum.Dimension.COLUMNS`
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Queue");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Queue" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("B2:C3").clear();
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("D2:D10").insertCells(univerAPI.Enum.Dimension.ROWS);
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return { success: true };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## AutoFill
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use `autoFill()` when a correct seed pattern already exists and the workbook should propagate it.
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `autoFill(destRange, fillType?) -> Promise<void>` - propagate from the source range into a larger destination that includes the source
[stderr] 
[stderr] Notes:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `destRange` must include the source range
[stderr] - `fillType` can be `'SERIES'` (default) or `'COPY'`
[stderr] - this method is async; use `await`
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] async () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Series");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Series" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   const source = sheet.getRange("A1:A2");
[stderr]   const dest = sheet.getRange("A1:A10");
[stderr]   await source.autoFill(dest);
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return { success: true, values: dest.getValues() };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Run-Specific Getchas
[stderr] 
[stderr] - all `run` code must be wrapped in an arrow function: `() => { ... }` or `async () => { ... }`
[stderr] - always return an object such as `{ success: true, ... }`; do not rely on implicit return values
[stderr] - only use methods explicitly documented in the run manual topics; do not guess method names or overloads
[stderr] - prefer `getSheetByName(...)` so the worksheet boundary stays explicit and reviewable
[stderr] - numeric coordinate overloads are 0-based, so `getRange(0, 0)` is `A1`
[stderr] - if `setValue('=...')` or `setFormula('=...')` writes a formula, wait for `await univerAPI.getFormula().onCalculationResultApplied()` before reading the computed result
[stderr] - `setValues()` requires a rectangular 2D array whose shape exactly matches the target range; `getRange(row, col)` without size only targets one cell
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Related Topics
[stderr] 
[stderr] - See `formulas` for a formula-first guide to calculation waits and readback.
[stderr] - See `formatting` when the job is primarily visual formatting instead of values or structure.
[stderr] - See `core` for `getRange()` variants, A1 notation, and coordinate rules.
[stderr] 
[stderr]  succeeded in 274ms:
[stderr] # Formulas
[stderr] 
[stderr] This topic focuses on formula writes, calculation waits, and readback behavior inside `run`.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## What This Topic Is For
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use this topic when the task needs workbook-native recalculation and the result must remain consistent with how the workbook computes formulas.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Formula Writes
[stderr] 
[stderr] You can write formulas with either of these APIs:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `setFormula(formula) -> void`
[stderr] - `setValue(value) -> FRange` when `value` starts with `=`
[stderr] 
[stderr] This applies to both:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `range.setFormula('=SUM(B1:B10)')`
[stderr] - `range.setValue('=SUM(B1:B10)')`
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Formula Calculation
[stderr] 
[stderr] Formula calculation is asynchronous. If you need the calculated result in the same `run`, wait for calculation to finish:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] await univerAPI.getFormula().onCalculationResultApplied();
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Available formula wait API:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `univerAPI.getFormula() -> FFormula`
[stderr] - `onCalculationResultApplied() -> Promise<void>` - wait for formula-calculation results to be applied before reading the computed value
[stderr] - note: if a real calculation runs, it resolves when results are applied; if no calculation starts quickly, it can resolve automatically instead of hanging forever
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Formula Warning
[stderr] 
[stderr] Without the wait, readback can be stale.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Copyable Example
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] async () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Sales");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Sales" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("A1").setFormula("=SUM(B1:B10)");
[stderr]   await univerAPI.getFormula().onCalculationResultApplied();
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return {
[stderr]     success: true,
[stderr]     formula: sheet.getRange("A1").getFormulas(),
[stderr]     value: sheet.getRange("A1").getValue()
[stderr]   };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Formula Readback With setValue()
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] async () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Sales");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Sales" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.getRange("A2").setValue("=SUM(B2:B10)");
[stderr]   await univerAPI.getFormula().onCalculationResultApplied();
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return {
[stderr]     success: true,
[stderr]     value: sheet.getRange("A2").getValue()
[stderr]   };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Getchas
[stderr] 
[stderr] - do not read the computed result immediately after writing a formula
[stderr] - always verify formula output with workbook-visible reads after the command
[stderr] - if the task is only propagation from an existing seed, prefer `ranges -> AutoFill` over custom formula scripting
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Related Topics
[stderr] 
[stderr] - See `ranges` for formula writes alongside value writes, shape rules, merges, clear, and AutoFill.
[stderr] - See `overview` for hard rules and command-selection guidance.
[stderr] 
[stderr] codex
[stderr] I can see the active sheet is `raw 1`, with classes in `F4:F8` and the existing count formula only in `G4`. The intended formula is a per-class count where `A` equals the row’s `F` value and either `B<>0` or `C<>0`; I’ll write that down `G4:G8` as formulas, then verify formulas and calculated displays.
[stderr] exec
[stderr] /bin/sh -lc 'univer help run sheets' in /task
[stderr] exec
[stderr] /bin/sh -lc 'univer help run core' in /task
[stderr] exec
[stderr] /bin/sh -lc 'univer inspect formulas /task/cases/case_1/input.univer' in /task

[stderr] # Sheets
[stderr] 
[stderr] This topic focuses on worksheet creation, worksheet structure, row or column presentation, freezing, gridlines, and visibility changes.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## What This Topic Is For
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use this topic when the task is about sheet-level or row/column-level structure instead of direct cell value changes.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Worksheet Lifecycle
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use the workbook object to locate, list, create, and delete sheets.
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `getSheetByName(name) -> FWorksheet | null` - recommended explicit lookup by sheet name
[stderr] - `getSheets() -> FWorksheet[]` - list all worksheets; useful when the task first needs sheet names
[stderr] - `create(name, rows, cols) -> FWorksheet` - create a new worksheet with an initial shape
[stderr] - `deleteSheet(sheetId) -> boolean` - delete the specified worksheet by sheet id
[stderr] - `getSheetId() -> number` - worksheet id
[stderr] - `getSheetName() -> string` - worksheet name
[stderr] - `setName(name) -> void` - rename the worksheet
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const existing = workbook.getSheetByName("Summary");
[stderr]   if (existing) {
[stderr]     existing.setName("Executive Summary");
[stderr]     return { success: true, sheetName: existing.getSheetName() };
[stderr]   }
[stderr] 
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.create("Summary", 100, 20);
[stderr]   return { success: true, sheetName: sheet.getSheetName() };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Rows And Columns
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `insertRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet` - insert rows at a 0-based starting index
[stderr] - `deleteRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet` - delete rows at a 0-based starting index
[stderr] - `insertColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet` - insert columns at a 0-based starting index
[stderr] - `deleteColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet` - delete columns at a 0-based starting index
[stderr] - `getLastRow() -> number` - 0-based index of the last row with data
[stderr] - `getLastColumn() -> number` - 0-based index of the last column with data
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Freeze Rows And Columns
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `setFrozenRows(rows)` or `setFrozenRows(startRow, endRow) -> FWorksheet` - freeze the first `rows` rows, or freeze a row range
[stderr] - `setFrozenColumns(columns)` or `setFrozenColumns(startColumn, endColumn) -> FWorksheet` - freeze the first `columns` columns, or freeze a column range
[stderr] 
[stderr] Insert rows and freeze the header:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Ops");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Ops" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.insertRows(1, 2);
[stderr]   sheet.setFrozenRows(1);
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return { success: true, lastRow: sheet.getLastRow() };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Gridlines
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `hasHiddenGridLines() -> boolean` - check whether gridlines are hidden
[stderr] - `setHiddenGridlines(hidden) -> FWorksheet` - show or hide sheet gridlines
[stderr] - `setGridLinesColor(color) -> FWorksheet` - set gridline color; `undefined` or `null` resets to default
[stderr] 
[stderr] Hide gridlines and set their color:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Dashboard");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Dashboard" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   sheet.setHiddenGridlines(true);
[stderr]   sheet.setGridLinesColor("#D9D9D9");
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return { success: true, hidden: sheet.hasHiddenGridLines() };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Row And Column Presentation
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `setRowHeight(rowIndex, height) -> FWorksheet` - set one row height in pixels
[stderr] - `setRowHeights(startRow, numRows, height) -> FWorksheet` - set multiple row heights
[stderr] - `showRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet` - show hidden rows
[stderr] - `hideRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet` - hide rows
[stderr] - `autoResizeRows(startRow, numRows) -> FWorksheet` - auto-size row heights to content
[stderr] - `setColumnWidth(columnIndex, width) -> FWorksheet` - set one column width in pixels
[stderr] - `setColumnWidths(startColumn, numColumns, width) -> FWorksheet` - set multiple column widths
[stderr] - `showColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet` - show hidden columns
[stderr] - `hideColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet` - hide columns
[stderr] - `autoResizeColumns(startColumn, numColumns) -> FWorksheet` - auto-size column widths to content
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Getchas
[stderr] 
[stderr] - imported workbooks can contain non-English worksheet names and still work normally
[stderr] - quote the full range string in the shell, for example `--range '工作表1!A1:J3'`
[stderr] - verify imported templates by reading a small anchor range and checking a few exact cells plus one non-empty cell
[stderr] - for workbook-visible structure checks, prefer `inspect workbook` over package metadata
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Related Topics
[stderr] 
[stderr] - See `core` for workbook entry points, `getRange()`, A1 notation, and coordinates.
[stderr] - See `formatting` for cell-level visual formatting after the sheet structure is in place.
[stderr] 
[stderr]  succeeded in 289ms:
[stderr] # Core
[stderr] 
[stderr] This topic groups the workbook, worksheet, and structural APIs behind most `run` tasks.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Object Hierarchy
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```text
[stderr] univerAPI
[stderr]   -> getActiveWorkbook() -> FWorkbook
[stderr]     -> getSheetByName(name) -> FWorksheet | null
[stderr]       -> getRange(...) -> FRange
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Entry Point
[stderr] 
[stderr] Start from the active workbook:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Top-level APIs used most often:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook() -> FWorkbook` - get the current active workbook
[stderr] - `univerAPI.getFormula() -> FFormula` - get the formula engine instance used for calculation synchronization
[stderr] 
[stderr] Dimension constants used by `insertCells()` and `deleteCells()`:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `univerAPI.Enum.Dimension.ROWS` - row dimension constant for `insertCells()` and `deleteCells()`
[stderr] - `univerAPI.Enum.Dimension.COLUMNS` - column dimension constant for `insertCells()` and `deleteCells()`
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Workbook And Worksheet Access
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use the workbook object to locate, list, create, and delete sheets.
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `getSheetByName(name) -> FWorksheet | null` - recommended explicit lookup by sheet name
[stderr] - `getSheets() -> FWorksheet[]` - list all worksheets; useful when the task first needs sheet names
[stderr] - `create(name, rows, cols) -> FWorksheet` - create a new worksheet with an initial shape
[stderr] - `deleteSheet(sheetId) -> boolean` - delete the specified worksheet by sheet id
[stderr] 
[stderr] Common worksheet reads:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `getSheetId() -> number`
[stderr] - `getSheetName() -> string`
[stderr] - `getLastRow() -> number` - 0-based index of the last row with data
[stderr] - `getLastColumn() -> number` - 0-based index of the last column with data
[stderr] - `hasHiddenGridLines() -> boolean`
[stderr] 
[stderr] Common worksheet writes:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `setName(name) -> void`
[stderr] - `insertRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `deleteRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `insertColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `deleteColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `setFrozenRows(rows)` or `setFrozenRows(startRow, endRow) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `setFrozenColumns(columns)` or `setFrozenColumns(startColumn, endColumn) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `setHiddenGridlines(hidden) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `setGridLinesColor(color) -> FWorksheet` - `undefined` or `null` resets to default
[stderr] - `setRowHeight(rowIndex, height) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `setRowHeights(startRow, numRows, height) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `showRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `hideRows(rowIndex, numRows) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `autoResizeRows(startRow, numRows) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `setColumnWidth(columnIndex, width) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `setColumnWidths(startColumn, numColumns, width) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `showColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `hideColumns(columnIndex, numColumns) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] - `autoResizeColumns(startColumn, numColumns) -> FWorksheet`
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const existing = workbook.getSheetByName("Summary");
[stderr]   if (existing) {
[stderr]     return { success: true, sheetName: existing.getSheetName() };
[stderr]   }
[stderr] 
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.create("Summary", 100, 20);
[stderr]   return { success: true, sheetName: sheet.getSheetName() };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] Formula wait API:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `onCalculationResultApplied() -> Promise<void>` - wait until formula-calculation results are applied before reading computed values
[stderr] - note: if a real calculation runs, it resolves when results are applied; if no calculation starts quickly, it can resolve automatically instead of hanging forever
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## getRange()
[stderr] 
[stderr] `getRange()` is the standard bridge from a worksheet to cell-level work.
[stderr] 
[stderr] Supported forms:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `getRange('A1')`
[stderr] - `getRange('A1:C10')`
[stderr] - `getRange('A:A')`
[stderr] - `getRange('1:1')`
[stderr] - `getRange(row, col)`
[stderr] - `getRange(row, col, rowCount, colCount)`
[stderr] 
[stderr] Example:
[stderr] 
[stderr] ```javascript
[stderr] () => {
[stderr]   const workbook = univerAPI.getActiveWorkbook();
[stderr]   const sheet = workbook.getSheetByName("Sales");
[stderr]   if (!sheet) return { success: false, error: 'Sheet "Sales" not found' };
[stderr] 
[stderr]   const header = sheet.getRange("A1:C1").getValues();
[stderr]   const firstCell = sheet.getRange(0, 0).getValue();
[stderr] 
[stderr]   return { success: true, header, firstCell };
[stderr] };
[stderr] ```
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Coordinate Model
[stderr] 
[stderr] Numeric coordinates are 0-based:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `getRange(0, 0)` is `A1`
[stderr] - `getRange(1, 2)` is `C2`
[stderr] - `getRange(0, 0, 2, 2)` is `A1:B2`
[stderr] - `insertRows(4, 3)` inserts 3 rows starting at spreadsheet row 5
[stderr] - `insertColumns(1, 2)` inserts 2 columns starting at spreadsheet column B
[stderr] 
[stderr] Use numeric coordinates for loops or generated positions. Use A1 notation when the target is fixed and workbook-facing.
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## A1 Notation
[stderr] 
[stderr] Prefer A1 notation for explicit workbook-facing locations:
[stderr] 
[stderr] - `'A1'`
[stderr] - `'A1:B10'`
[stderr] - `'A:A'`
[stderr] - `'1:1'`
[stderr] 
[stderr] ## Related Topics
[stderr] 
[stderr] - See `sheets` for worksheet-focused structure and presentation tasks.
[stderr] - See `ranges` for cell reads, writes, formulas, merges, clear, cell shifts, and AutoFill.
[stderr] - See `overview` when you need the command shape, execution rules, and hard rules.
[stderr]
